The cysteine (C) in this motif serves as the catalytic nucleophile, attacking the carbonyl carbon of the peptide substrate.<br>- The &quot;HGKWYKM&quot; motif contains the catalytic histidine (H), which acts as the general base/proton acceptor — it abstracts a proton from the cysteine thiol to generate a thiolate anion (a stronger nucleophile).<br>- There is also a glutamine (Q) residue that helps orient the histidine, completing the Cys-His-Gln catalytic triad.<br><br>This triad arrangement (Cys-His-Gln) is the classic catalytic mechanism of papain-like cysteine proteases:<br>- **Cysteine** → nucleophile (forms a covalent thioester intermediate with the substrate)<br>- **Histidine** → proton acceptor (general base that deprotonates the cysteine)<br><br>Evaluating the options:<br>- A: Serine-His is the catalytic dyad/triad of serine proteases (e.g., trypsin), not applicable here.<br>- B: Cys-Asp is not the typical combination for this enzyme family.<br>- C: This reverses the roles incorrectly.<br>- D: Cys as nucleophile, His as proton acceptor — this matches the known mechanism of papain-like cysteine proteases.<br><br>2. answer: D</td>

Mitochondrial inner membrane, with multiple membrane-spanning segments - Mitochondrial proteins typically have an N-terminal mitochondrial targeting sequence (amphipathic helix, rich in arginine and hydroxylated amino acids, poor in acidic residues). C. Plasma membrane as a multipass membrane protein - We don&#x27;t see multiple membrane-spanning segments. There&#x27;s mainly one clear hydrophobic stretch.

D. Golgi membrane as a single-pass membrane protein - While possible for a protein that passes through the ER and Golgi, the signal peptide + single TM domain is most consistent with an ER resident or ER-associated protein. Golgi proteins typically have a single TM domain but the signal peptide would have been processed, and the localization would depend on retention signals.

The combination of:

- A single transmembrane domain
- The topology being consistent with a type I membrane protein
